TI Dielectric Polymer Based Tunable Bandpass Filter for RF MEMS Applications
SO JOURNAL OF POLYMER & COMPOSITES
LA English
DT Article
DE Coplanar waveguide (CPW); dielectric polymers; Distributed MEMS transmission layer; electromagnetic simulations; MEMS switches; parametric studies; reconfigurability; RF transmission
ID material selection; center frequency; microwave; compact; design
AB In this paper, we report on the design, modeling, and analysis of a tunable bandpass filter implemented in a coplanar waveguide (CPW) configuration, where a distributed MEMS transmission line (DMTL) is integrated with dielectric polymer layers to achieve dynamic frequency and bandwidth reconfigurability. The proposed filter architecture leverages the tunable dielectric response ofpolymer films, enabling precise control of the center frequency and passband width under applied electrostatic bias. Comprehensive parametric studies were conducted on polymer characteristics-including dielectric constant, material selection, film thickness, surface uniformity, and air-gap geometry-to optimize capacitance variation, minimize actuation voltage, and maintain consistent RF stability. Electromagnetic simulations validated that the dielectric polymer integration allows center frequency tuning from 0.2 to 1 GHz, bandwidth reconfiguration of approximately 0.7 GHz, and superior microwave performance, characterized by a return loss of-22.5 dB and insertion loss near-3 dB. The fabrication process is compatible with low-temperature processing and flexible substrates, offering excellent scalability for compact and conformal microwave systems. Owing to its reconfigurable nature and reliable performance, the proposed dielectric polymer-based RF MEMS bandpass filter holds significant potential for next-generation UHF, radar, and consumer wireless communication applications.

TI Effect of Frequency-Dependent Behavior of a Gap-Filler Dielectric Elastomer on Nonlinear Dynamics of Capacitive MEMS
SO INTERNATIONAL JOURNAL OF ENGINEERING
LA English
DT Article
DE Sensor; Soft Material; Modeling; Microplate
ID modulus
AB This study explores the use of high-kappa soft materials as gap-fillers in capacitive MEMS, Afocusing on the impact of frequency-dependent dielectric constants on system performance. A circular capacitive microplate with a porous dielectric elastomer, whose dielectric constant follows the Havriliak-Negami relation, is analyzed. The elastomer's Young's modulus varies with displacement due to displacement-dependent porosity, introducing additional nonlinearities. The nonlinear frequency response of the system is subsequently analyzed using a continuation-based extended energy balance method with an arc-length constraint, which predicts all harmonic components, including primary and secondary resonances, as well as isolas. The results of this study demonstrate that incorporating the frequency-dependent dielectric constant alters the frequency response by increasing the fundamental frequency and reducing vibration amplitudes compared to models assuming constant or relaxed permittivity. Furthermore, the study underscores that the system's nonlinear behavior can transition from softening to hardening when the frequency-dependent dielectric constant is taken into account, underscoring the importance of accurate modeling of dielectric behavior for the prediction of MEMS performance.

TI Research on the Stability of Soft Rock Slope in Open-pit Coal Mine Under Multi-source Vibration
SO INDIAN GEOTECHNICAL JOURNAL
LA English
DT Article; Early Access
DE Open-pit coal mine; Multi-source dynamic disturbance; Numerical simulation; Vibration monitoring; Slope stability
AB To elucidate the influence mechanism of multi-source dynamic disturbances on slope stability in open-pit coal mines, this study focuses on the west slope of the first mining area in Hesigewula South Open-pit Coal Mine. A blasting vibration monitoring system (with MEMS triaxial accelerometers and a cloud-based platform) was deployed from January 1 to June 28, 2025, recording 740 vibration events. Dynamic parameters including vibration duration, peak acceleration, peak velocity, amplitude, and dominant frequency were systematically analyzed. A Geo-slope numerical model incorporating lithological weak planes and hydrogeological conditions was established based on the Mohr-Coulomb constitutive model, simulating 19 typical vibration scenarios. The results indicate: (1) Vibration durations ranged from 78 to 9448 ms, with peak accelerations up to 92.472 mg (Z-direction), peak velocities up to 1.908 cm/s, maximum amplitude of 31.019 cm (Y-direction), and dominant frequencies concentrated between 20 and 60 Hz. (2) Numerical simulations show that vibration accelerations significantly reduce the slope stability factor, with the lowest value of 1.161 under the scenario of June 5, 2025 (horizontal 30.184 mg, vertical 25.833 mg). (3) The vibration peak acceleration increased significantly during the summer construction peak, and the impact on the stability of the slope was intensified. Based on the analysis between vibration parameters and stability coefficients, it is recommended to set a threshold value of vibration acceleration (e.g., > 20 mg) as an early warning indicator, to control the distance of vibration source, blasting quantity value, etc., so as to achieve active prevention and control of the slope under disturbance. This study provides theoretical and support for the monitoring and prevention of dynamic disasters of open-pit coal mine slopes. .

TI Assessment of Xsens Motion Trackers' Accuracy to Measure Induced Vibrations During Endurance Running
SO JOURNAL OF FUNCTIONAL MORPHOLOGY AND KINESIOLOGY
LA English
DT Article
DE human response to vibration monitoring; long-distance running; MEMS; IMU for sport applications; wearable devices; running-related injuries (RRIs); endurance training
ID soft-tissue vibrations; long-distance runners; muscle-activity; injury; leg; body
AB Background: Research on vibrations induced by running has gained significant attention due to its implications for athletes' performance, injury prevention, and overall well-being. Distance running exposes the body to repetitive impulsive forces, causing significant vibrations to travel through physiological systems and biomechanical structures. These vibrations increase fatigue and the risk of injury. Although it has gained importance, research on induced vibration during running and wearable equipment for monitoring is scarce. This study aims to evaluate the performance of a measurement system for monitoring the acceleration levels of induced vibrations during long-distance running, exploring the capability of non-invasive wearable devices to characterise vibration transmissibility and exposure. Moreover, a preliminary quantitative assessment of induced vibration levels for an indoor testing scenario is given. Methods: Metrological characterisation of Xsens Motion Trackers Awinda (MTw), off-the-shelf inertial magnetic motion trackers, was performed by measuring the sensors' frequency bandwidth in a controlled environment, providing logarithmic sweep sine excitations at different levels (2 g, 5 g, 7 g, where g is meant to be the gravitational acceleration). A testing protocol for indoor testing was derived from the literature, allowing characterisation of the sensors' behaviour in terms of vibration transmissibility and exposure detection in the intended application. Time domain and frequency domain analyses were conducted by following the ISO 2631 standard guideline for vibration exposure assessment, and measurement uncertainty was defined, either for the dynamic correction of the sensors' frequency behaviour or for the computed time and frequency domain metrics. In this framework, a treadmill-based test was conducted. The aim was to evaluate the Xsens sensors' performance in measuring vibration dose exposure and transmissibility. Three MTws were placed on the subject's right tibia, back, and forehead using elastic bands. A 25-year-old female amateur runner completed a series of tests consisting of walking for 1 min at 3.5 km/h (instrumentation setup), followed by running at two speeds (8 km/h and 11 km/h) for 2-4 min per trial, with 5 min rest periods between tests. Conclusions: The tested measurement system showed promising results due to its capability to assess vibration exposure during sports activities, but dynamic correction was found to be mandatory for accurate vibration level assessment. The main outcome of this study is a method for characterising the accelerometers embedded in the proposed devices, along with an analysis strategy for future testing campaigns. Thanks to the portability of IMUs (inertial measurement units), this approach enables the evaluation of induced vibrations during in-field running measurements.

TI DAWN: Efficient Distribution of Attention Workload in PIM-Enabled Systems for LLM Inference
SO IEEE COMPUTER ARCHITECTURE LETTERS
LA English
DT Article
DE Vectors; Resource management; Layout; Throughput; Bandwidth; Load management; Indexes; Electronic mail; Microelectromechanical systems; Memory management; Processing-in-memory (PIM); self-attention
AB Recently, processing-in-memory (PIM) units have been deployed to accelerate matrix-vector multiplications in large language models (LLMs). However, due to the limited flexibility of PIMs, PIMs require a strict data layout for storing matrices in memory. As LLM inference operates autoregressively, new elements are appended to the stored matrices during inference, necessitating costly data layout reorganization. Nevertheless, since the conventional workload allocation method assigns entire matrices solely to PIMs, it causes data layout reorganization overhead (i.e., excessive memory writes). Furthermore, the significant variance in matrix sizes exacerbates PIM load imbalance. In this letter, we propose DAWN, a novel workload allocation method. DAWN divides matrices into equally sized chunks and employs a single chunk as the allocation unit. DAWN assigns a portion of chunks to traditional accelerators (e.g., neural processing units), which have no constraints on data layout for computation, to mitigate reorganization overhead. DAWN evenly distributes the remaining chunks across PIMs using a greedy approach to achieve PIM load balancing. Our simulation results show that DAWN improves throughput by up to 44.2% (34.8% on average) over the conventional workload allocation method.

TI Graphene-based origami with bidirectional bending and folding
SO NANO TODAY
LA English
DT Article
DE 3D microfabrication; Origami; Graphene; Bidirectional bending; Folding
ID strength
AB The precise fabrication and deformation of three-dimensional (3D) microstructures, such as origami and kirigami with folded features typically ranging from a few to several tens of micrometers, have gained significant interest owing to their versatility in advanced microfabrication processes. However, conventional approaches relying on flexible polymers or thin metals face limitations, such as unidirectional bending and poor spatial resolution in localized deformation. Here, we present a strategy to construct graphene-based origami structures by harnessing the mechanical properties of graphene and exploiting electron beam (e-beam)-induced deformation of graphenepolymer double layer. Poly(methyl methacrylate) (PMMA)/graphene bilayer films exhibit a significant shift in the neutral axis due to the high in-plane stiffness of graphene, enabling controlled bidirectional bending under selective e-beam irradiation. By sequential e-beam exposure on PMMA-based structures with spatially patterned graphene, we achieve complex 3D geometries, including flower- and crown-like motifs, as well as folding mechanisms such as chair-like pop-up designs and box-shaped enclosures. Furthermore, we demonstrate rotational motion in wheel-shaped structures, translating out-of-plane bending into in-plane rotation by the shortening effect. Our approach expands the design freedom and functional capabilities of microfabricated systems, offering a powerful platform for programmable, reconfigurable 3D architectures in microelectromechanical systems (MEMS), robotics, and soft materials.

TI Mechanics analysis of support loss mechanisms in flexible MEMS resonators
SO JOURNAL OF SOUND AND VIBRATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Flexible electronics; Microbeam resonator; Support loss; Viscoelastic substrate; Dimensionless analysis
ID viscoelastic characterization; instrumented indentation; beam resonators; anchor
AB With recent advances in flexible electronics technology, flexible micro-electro-mechanical systems (MEMS) resonators have attracted increasing attention. When operating within deformable environments, these resonators exhibit behaviors distinct from those mounted on rigid/linearelastic substrates. The substrates used in flexible MEMS resonators are typically polymer-based materials, which often exhibit viscoelastic properties. Consequently, understanding the support loss and its underlying mechanisms in viscoelastic substrates is critical for the effective design of flexible MEMS resonators. This study adopts a generalized Maxwell viscoelastic model in conjunction with Euler-Bernoulli beam theory and applies the Fourier transform to derive the substrate dynamic response and the associated stress-strain relations relevant to support loss evaluation under harmonic loading transmitted through the supports. The validity of the analytical solution is further verified via finite element analysis using the perfectly matched layer technique. A dimensionless analysis is performed to examine the effects of geometric parameters of the resonator and support, as well as the material parameters of both the resonator and substrate. The quality factors of resonators on polymer-based flexible substrates and on conventional linear-elastic substrates are compared. The results indicate that the effect of viscosity becomes more pronounced as the vibration frequency decreases. These findings offer valuable insights for the design and implementation of flexible MEMS resonators.

TI Stress-relaxation-induced enhancement of piezoelectric response in 0.7Pb(Mg1/3Nb2/3)O3-0.3PbTiO3</sub > thin films
SO CERAMICS INTERNATIONAL
LA English
DT Article
DE PMN-PT; Ferroelectric thin film; Sol-gel; Buffer layer; Flexible substrate
ID leakage current; phase
AB High-performance piezoelectric thin films are critical for next-generation microelectromechanical systems and flexible electronic devices, yet the properties are often limited by lattice mismatch/thermal treating induced stress. Here, stress in the sol-gel prepared 0.7 Pb(Mg1/3Nb2/3)O-3-0.3PbTiO(3) (PMN-PT) thin film was partially released by using a LaNiO3-buffered Mica substrate, yielding a substantial enhancement in piezoelectric performance. X-ray diffraction analysis showed that the 100 diffraction peak of the PMN-PT film grown on LaNiO3/Mica shifted to a higher angle and was closer to the intrinsic 100 peak position of PMN-PT, indicating substantial relaxation of the substrate-induced in-plane compressive stress. Meanwhile, this substrate promoted highly 100-oriented growth with a texture fraction of 97.9%, lowered the coercive field to 8.9 kV/cm, and reduced the leakage current to below 10(-8) A. Importantly, the effective piezoelectric coefficient d(33)& lowast; increased from similar to 85 p.m./V on the rigid Pt/Si substrate to similar to 121 p.m./V, corresponding to an enhancement of similar to 42%. The film further exhibited robust and reversible ferroelectric domain switching with excellent polarization stability after 10(6) switching cycles. These results demonstrate an efficient substrate-engineering route for optimizing ferroelectric thin-film performance and provide a practical foundation for developing flexible, fatigue-resistant, and high-efficiency piezoelectric devices.

TI On-Site Estimation of Peak Ground Acceleration Using the S/P Amplitude Ratio for MEMS-Based Earthquake Early Warning Systems in Iasi, Romania
SO SAFETY
LA English
DT Article
DE S/P amplitude ratio; peak ground acceleration; Earthquake Early Warning (EEW); on-site estimation; MEMS accelerometer; local amplification; soft-soil response
ID taiwan
AB This study presents a site-specific calibration of the ratio between S-wave and P-wave peak ground acceleration (PGA) for use in low-cost, on-site earthquake early warning (EEWS) systems in Iasi, Romania. A dataset of 25 intermediate-depth Vrancea earthquakes (Mw 4.1-5.7; epicentral distances 150-210 km) was analyzed. PGA values were extracted for the P- and S-wave windows on both horizontal components and combined using geometric means. The resulting S/P amplitude ratios yield a median value of kS/P = 6.19 and a logarithmic standard deviation of sigma log10 = 0.31, corresponding to a multiplicative uncertainty factor of approximately & times;2. These results indicate that S-wave amplitudes are typically six times larger than P-wave amplitudes at this site, consistent with soft-soil amplification observed in comparable stations in Japan and Italy. The calibrated ratio can be used as a site-specific input for future MEMS-based on-site EEW implementations to estimate the expected S-wave PGA immediately after P-wave detection, with the observed S-P delays in Iasi indicating a typical available warning window of 20-22 s.

TI Characterization of crystallized thick, highly doped PECVD a-Si:H layers for fabrication of surface micromachined MEMS inertial sensors
SO JOURNAL OF MICROMECHANICS AND MICROENGINEERING
LA English
DT Article
DE PECVD; crystallization; a-Si:H
ID silicon; films
AB This study evaluates the feasibility of thick crystallized amorphous silicon (14-21 & micro;m) layers deposited by a plasma-enhanced chemical vapor deposition process (PECVD) as a structural material for the realization of MEMS inertial sensors. The aim of this study was to evaluate the thickness uniformity, sheet resistance uniformity, and mechanical stress characteristics of crystallized amorphous silicon. The fabrication process involves the deposition of amorphous silicon layers using PECVD processes with PH3:H-2 flow rates of 200 sccm and 500 sccm, followed by controlled crystallization through annealing at temperatures ranging from 750 degrees C to 850 degrees C. Both sacrificial silicon oxide and structural amorphous silicon layers are processed on a single PECVD equipment platform. Characterization techniques include wafer-level thickness measurements, sheet resistance mapping, and stress-deflection analysis to identify relations between the process and the material properties of the crystallized a-Si:H films. The results show that 200 sccm PH3:H-2 flow rate delivers significantly superior thickness and sheet resistance uniformity compared to 500 sccm conditions. Crystallization temperatures above 800 degrees C substantially improve the uniformities on the wafer and enable a precise stress and stress-deflection tuning. The crystallized material exhibits nearly isotropic characteristics with a controllable transition from tensile to compressive stress by adjusting the crystallization temperature. Near-zero stress conditions are achieved for the samples deposited with 200 sccm PH3:H-2 addition at temperatures between 750 degrees C and 800 degrees C. Additionally, chemical mechanical planarization and deep reactive ion etching processing times are notably reduced while maintaining excellent compatibility with established micro surface micromachining processes. In conclusion, the new crystallized PECVD amorphous silicon process offers a viable and advantageous alternative to epitaxial polysilicon, providing superior uniformity and valuable engineering flexibility for the design of stress-compensated devices for high-volume manufacturing of consumer inertial sensors.

TI Enhanced sensitivity in capacitive micro-machined ultrasound transducers using hydrogel soft dielectric materials for implantable biomedical applications
SO RESULTS IN ENGINEERING
LA English
DT Article
DE Bio implantable sensors; MEMS; Capacitive; Nonlinear; Hydrogel; Ultrasound
ID mems
AB This work introduces a nonlinear dynamic model for an advanced capacitive micro-machined ultrasound transducer (CMUT) incorporating hydrogel-based soft dielectric materials for implantable biomedical sensing. The system, modeled as a coupled micro-plate (MP) and finite-thickness dielectric layer (DL), is subjected to electrostatic and harmonic excitations. The hydrogel dielectric, characterized by high permittivity and low Young's modulus, is analyzed using nonlinear strain theory and the elasto-dynamic Navier-Cauchy framework. Governing equations are discretized via Hilbert-space expansions and solved using a Physically Gradient-Descent-Based Learning Method (PGDBLM), enabling a precise frequency-domain analysis. Key results demonstrate a substantial 96% reduction in pull-in voltage (PV), from 66.64 V (air dielectric) to 2.70 V (PD9), highlighting enhanced electrostatic coupling for ultra-low-voltage operation. Frequency response analysis reveals significant damping effects, making this configuration particularly suitable for quasi-static physiological monitoring (such as intracranial/bladder pressure) rather than high-frequency ultrasound applications. To address hydrogel swelling, encapsulation strategies are proposed. This work establishes a foundational model for designing hydrogel-enhanced CMUTs, providing critical insights into performance trade-offs and a framework for future development of specialized low-power biomedical sensors through material optimization and experimental validation.

TI Physics-Guided Machine Learning for Enhanced Piezoelectric Performance in Electrospun PVDF/SnO2 Nanofibers
SO SUSTAINABLE MATERIALS AND TECHNOLOGIES
LA English
DT Article
DE Electrospinning; Query attention; Piezoelectric nanofibers; Energy harvesting; MEMS actuation; SnO2 doping
ID thermal-conductivity
AB Sustainable piezoelectric design linking processing and microstructure with device performance is needed for wearable energy harvesters and soft actuators. NLP was used for gap analysis to identify SnO2 as a high-potential yet underexplored filler for PVDF. To address the literature data scarcity, we developed a Physics-Guided Machine Learning (PGMLpiezo) model that provides property predictions for multiscale FEM device simulations. Using optimized electrospinning (18 w/v% PVDF), SnO2 doping reduced fiber diameter from 680 nm to 290 nm. The /1-phase fraction increased from 0.3456 (powder) to 0.5725. The PGMLpiezo model predicts a d33 increase from 18 to 28.91 pC/N after the addition of SnO2 in PVDF. Using these predicted properties, FEM simulations were performed to evaluate device-level performance, showing thickness-mode harvesting with open-circuit voltages of 4.7 V and nano-watt range power outputs. Similarly, a lateral-clamp actuation gives strokes up to 69.6 nm with an effective deff 33 = 45.95 pm/V.

TI Towards higher throughput laser processing via high-speed beamforming and patterning
SO HIGH-POWER LASER MATERIALS PROCESSING: APPLICATIONS, DIAGNOSTICS, AND SYSTEMS XV
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
DE MEMS; Spatial Light Modulator; Planar Light Valve (PLV (TM)); Displacement Phase Modulator (DPM (TM)); diffractive optics; amplitude modulation; phase modulation; laser material processing
AB Laser-based manufacturing has become a cornerstone of high- precision fabrication processes, often offering more flexibility and cleanliness compared to other mechanical or chemical processes. Conventional systems typically employ a Gaussian beam steered via galvanometer mirrors and focused onto the workpiece through an f-theta lens. While this configuration provides robust performance for many applications, its inherent limitations-such as static beam shape and constrained laser power-have spurred significant research into adaptive beam shaping and high-power laser utilization. This paper explores the implementation of high-speed MEMS modulators to fully take advantage of the ready availability of increasing laser power and compares two approaches using amplitude or phase modulation, respectively. For pattern dense and cosmetically challenging applications, we demonstrate a system using an amplitude-modulating planar light valve (PLV (TM)) to finely control irradiance distribution at the work surface, enabling enhanced precision and reduced thermal side effects. We will cover the latest developments of our high-speed laser processing head, highlighting recent advancements in scanning throughput and system integration. For highly flexible beamforming a displacement phase modulator (DPM (TM)) is integrated into the scanning path to dynamically tailor the phase profile of the beam during vector scanning operations. By modulating phase on-the-fly, the system can produce beam profiles optimized for specific materials, geometries, or process outcomes at each point along a scan path. Both the amplitude-modulating PLV and the phase-modulating DPM demonstrate compatibility with high-power laser sources, opening avenues for their application in throughput-critical and next-generation manufacturing platforms. These devices significantly extend the capability of laser processing, enabling programmable control over spatial and temporal energy deposition for customized, high-efficiency fabrication.

TI A cost-effective laboratory device for single slow micro compression testing of soft materials in the small-strain region
SO HARDWAREX
LA English
DT Article
DE Micro-compression testing; Mechanical properties; Cost-effective instrumentation; Small strain region; Laboratory devices
ID mechanical characterization; polydimethylsiloxane pdms; nanoindentation; fabrication; tensile; design; behaviors
AB In various microscale applications, accurate evaluation of the mechanical properties of materials under small-strain and low-force conditions is important. However, conventional universal testing machines (UTM) are expensive and difficult to operate reliably under low-force conditions, making them unsuitable for small-strain testing. To overcome these limitations, we developed a cost-effective device for single slow micro-compression testing (MCT) to measure the mechanical properties of materials in the small-strain region. The MCT consists of a force sensor (FlexiForce A301-1, Tekscan, USA), an Arduino-based signal acquisition module, and a high-precision z-stage. The displacement control accuracy of the z-stage was verified using a laser displacement meter (LK-G10, Keyence, Japan), and the experimental results confirmed displacement and force resolutions of 1 mu m and 0.01 N, respectively. Under no-load conditions, the force-displacement error between the MCT and a commercial universal testing machine (MTS, AMETEK LRX-plus, LLOYD INSTRUMENTS, UK) was within +/- 2.0 %. In addition, standard and one-third-size PDMS (polydimethylsiloxane) specimens were fabricated according to ASTM D575-91 and tested using both systems; the error between the two specimens was within +/- 0.05 %. From the linear region in the small-strain range, the Young's modulus of the miniature specimen was estimated, showing a percentage error of + 2.2 % from reported values, confirming high precision and reliability. The developed device, with a total fabrication cost below USD 1,500, provides costeffectiveness, precision, and repeatability, enabling applications in polymer MEMS and soft robotics.

TI A Translational Platform for Polyimide Neural Interfaces: Polyimide Synthesis and in Vivo Evaluation in Epileptic Mice
SO IEEE ACCESS
LA English
DT Article
DE Polyimides; Electrodes; Recording; Epilepsy; Mice; Probes; Thermal stability; Surface roughness; Rough surfaces; Monitoring; Neural interfaces; polyimide thin films; microelectrode arrays; MEMS fabrication; biocompatibility; epilepsy monitoring
ID thin-films; biocompatibility; array; stimulation; implants; probe
AB Thin-film polyimide neural probes have shown great promise in neuroscience but remain difficult to clinically translate due to the unavailability and lack of customizability of commercially available medical-grade polyamic acids. We present an open-source, end-to-end platform for synthesizing BPDA-pPDA-based polyimide from a custom polyamic acid and translating it into microfabricated thin-film neural interfaces. The approach combines accessible polymer chemistry with a streamlined MEMS-compatible fabrication process to produce flexible, biocompatible depth and surface electrode arrays with high thermal stability, chemical inertness, and low moisture uptake. Devices were validated through benchtop characterization, ISO 10993-11 systemic toxicity testing, and in vivo electrophysiology, both acute and semi-chronic, in wild-type and laforin knockout epileptic mice. The arrays reliably captured high-quality multi- and single-unit activity, as well as spontaneous epileptiform discharges, over implantation periods of up to 12 days. By demonstrating a customizable, end-to-end platform for synthesizing and fabricating thin-film polyimide neural electrodes, and by mimicking human neurosurgical workflows through depth, surface, and semi-chronic studies in mice, this work underscores the translational potential of polyimide-based neural microelectrodes and provides a practical pathway to accelerate clinical adoption.

TI Acquisition Strategies for Free-Space Optical Links Using MEMS Phase Light Modulators
SO FREE-SPACE LASER COMMUNICATIONS XXXVIII
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
DE Free space optical communications; non-mechanical beam scanning; Acquisition and tracking; Phase light modulator; Beam steering; MEMS
AB There is growing interest in deploying autonomous free-space optical (FSO) communication links for applications ranging from low earth orbit (LEO) constellations to underwater imaging. To improve the size, weight, power, and cost (SWaPc) of these systems, various optical architectures are being explored. Our group has developed an FSO communication system prototype that eliminates the need for deformable mirrors and bulky beam-steering mirrors by using a MEMS-based Phase Light Modulator (PLM). In this work, we present acquisition and tracking strategies specifically designed for autonomous operation of these devices. The system uses a PLM and detector at both the transmitter and receiver to enable dynamic beam shaping and steering. This flexibility simplifies the overall system and supports rapid and reliable link acquisition in a variety of free-space environments.

TI Scaled 3D-printed models of insect outer-ear with tympanic membranes and acoustic trachea preserving key acoustic features
SO COMPUTATIONAL AND STRUCTURAL BIOTECHNOLOGY JOURNAL
LA English
DT Article
DE Bioacoustics; Biomimetic replication; Bush-crickets; Deep segmentation; Laser-Doppler vibrometer; Micro-computed tomography
ID orthoptera; evolution; hearing
AB Katydids (Insecta, Orthoptera, Tettigoniidae) possess a sophisticated foreleg ear with two tympana receiving sound externally through a pinna that acts as a bat detector and internally through an acoustic trachea for conspecific communication. Their miniature scale hinders experimentation, prompting the use of scaled models, yet previous studies have not replicated the full pinna-tympana assembly or the complete acoustic trachea. In this study, micro-CT imaging, AI-assisted segmentation and multi-material 3D-printed assembly were used to generate scaled models. Scaled copies of the pinna-tympanum assembly and the complete acoustic trachea of the neotropical katydid Copiphora gorgonensis were fabricated from high-fidelity reconstructions. Flexible TPU membranes reproduced the expected pressure-driven vibration pattern at the scaled frequency, and when paired with rigid PLA pinnae, they captured the overall outer-ear acoustic response, producing ultrasonic gain within 70-110 kHz that is consistent with the in vivo bat-detection band. Separately, the pressure mapping of the scaled acoustic trachea confirms the spiracle as a filter and the exponential canal as a 17-21 dB amplifier, in line with simulations, consistent with the 1.3-cycle phase shift observed at 23 kHz in living insects. These matching results support the use of scaled biomimetic replicas as reusable, 3Rs-aligned partial substitutes and complements to live-insect acoustic studies in search of bio-inspired applications. Taken together, these outcomes provide a basis for translating the underlying acoustic and structural principles into future miniature engineering systems, including bio-inspired MEMS microphones and compact directional sensors, where passive amplification and filtering remain desirable for improving energy efficiency and signal clarity.

TI Electromagnetically driven 4x4 micromirror arrays based on FPCB
SO 11TH INTERNATIONAL SYMPOSIUM ON ADVANCED OPTICAL MANUFACTURING AND TESTING TECHNOLOGIES, AOMATT 2025, PT 1
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
DE electromagnetic-driven; 4x4micromirror array; FPCB driving structure
ID adaptive optics; mems
AB This paper proposes an electromagnetic-driven micromirror array based on a flexible printed circuit board (FPCB). The design mainly consists of three parts: a silicon mirror, an FPCB driving structure, and a permanent magnet. The silicon mirror is attached to the FPCB driving structure. By adjusting the current magnitude and direction in the coil of the FPCB, the magnitude and direction of the magnetic force between the coil and the permanent magnet can be changed, which induces mechanical deformation of the driving structure and thus realizes the deflection and translation of the mirror. Experimental results show that the fabricated 4x4 micromirror array has a unit static resistance of approximately 9 ohm, a resonant frequency of 138Hz, and a fill factor of 85.5%. Under a driving voltage of +/- 2.5V and an operating current of 200mA, each unit can achieve an optical deflection angle of more than +/- 28 degrees. This study verifies the feasibility of using FPCB as the driving structure.

TI Unsteady surface pressure measurements of propeller-wing interaction with a MEMS-embedded sleeve
SO AEROSPACE SCIENCE AND TECHNOLOGY
LA English
DT Article
DE MEMS; Unsteady pressure measurement; Propeller-wing interaction
ID laminar separation-bubbles; boundary-layer; flow
AB This work investigates the unsteady aerodynamic interaction that arises from the impingement of a propeller slipstream on a wing. To this end, an innovative measuring device for unsteady pressure is deployed, comprising a flexible printed circuit board sleeve embedded with MEMS pressure sensors and microphones. The device performance is validated against conventional measurement techniques. The wing is a benchmarked NACA 633018 airfoil-based model, and the propeller is the TUD-XPROP-S. In addition to pressure measurements, oil flow visualizations are performed to elucidate the flow pattern on the wing when the propeller operates at advance ratios of 0.8 and 1.8, and nominal blade pitch angles of 30 degrees and 45 degrees. The measurements reveal the formation of a laminar separation bubble on the portion of the wing not washed by the propeller slipstream. The flow is seen to remain attached on the advancing blade side, at least for the tested angles of attack. The microphone measurements capture the trace of the propeller's tip vortices over the wing and the deformation of the slipstream over the wing. This work serves a dual purpose. Firstly, presenting an innovative measuring device for unsteady pressure, as the sensor-embedded sleeve requires minimal installation efforts and allows for a comprehensive measurement of the unsteady surface pressure field. Secondly, discussing the complex spatio temporal interaction that is formed from the impingement of a propeller slipstream onto a wing.

TI PiezoMEMS Fabrication on Flexible Stainless-Steel Substrates
SO SENSORS
LA English
DT Article
DE flexible MEMS; PMUT; PZT; metal foil
ID piezoelectric coefficient; thin-films; e(31,f); sensor
AB A bottom-up fabrication approach for flexible piezoelectric micromachined ultrasound transducer (PMUT) arrays on stainless-steel substrates was developed. Devices were fabricated using chemical solution deposition of a 700 nm-thick layer of Pb-0.99 square(0.01)(Zr0.52Ti0.48)Nb0.02O3, where square denotes a vacancy on the Pb site, on 50 mu m-thick LaNiO3/HfO2/stainless-steel foils. Lithography for definition of the electrode and piezoelectric layers was completed on the front of the wafer. Ni electroplating on the back side of the foil was used to create locally stiff areas to define the deflection area. PMUT devices were successfully fabricated using this method. The permittivity and loss tangent of the fabricated device at 1 kHz were 283 +/- 9 and <1.5%, respectively. The remanent polarization was measured to be 38 +/- 0.3 mu C/cm(2).

TI Finite element buckling analysis for micro structures based on the couple stress theory
SO THIN-WALLED STRUCTURES
LA English
DT Article
DE Buckling analysis; Size effect; Finite element method; Couple stress theory
ID behavior; plates
AB Mechanical behavior at the micro- and nano-scale exhibits size-dependent effects, such as increased stiffness, which are not captured by classical continuum mechanics. These effects become particularly important in thin structures, where buckling is a critical failure mode; however, previous studies have been limited to simple geometries due to the complexity of the governing equations. This study develops a finite element framework for buckling analysis based on the modified couple stress theory (MCST), applicable to arbitrary geometries. Beam and shell elements are formulated to incorporate size effects, enabling buckling analysis of general structures. The framework is demonstrated on various configurations, including thin membranes under residual stress, stiffened plates, and tensile bars, highlighting its versatility. Numerical results show that accounting for size effects consistently increases the predicted critical buckling loads compared to classical continuum mechanics. The proposed approach offers a broadly applicable tool for stability assessment in micro-electromechanical systems (MEMS), flexible electronics, and thin-film micro sensors and actuators.

TI A wireless and passive multiplexable biomarker sensing system constructed entirely on a soft and flexible thin film
SO SENSORS AND ACTUATORS B-CHEMICAL
LA English
DT Article
DE Microelectromechanical systems (MEMS); Thin film sensor; Wireless sensor; Passive sensor; Electrochemical sensor
ID glucose; sensors; sodium; phenylalanine; csf
AB Continuous biomarker monitoring devices are poised to advance personalized medicine; however current devices are largely dependent on rigid, silicon-based electronics for signal processing and data transmission. In this work, we demonstrate a wireless and passive multiplexable biomarker sensing system constructed solely of biocompatible and thin, flexible films. Devices were realized through standard polymer microfabrication techniques and perform passive wireless electrochemical measurement of biomarker concentrations using the principle of reflected impedance. To demonstrate the wide range of electrochemical sensing mechanisms suitable for the device, four commonly used but fundamentally distinct electrochemical sensing modes were selected for demonstration. Specifically, detection of glucose using enzyme, cortisol using antibody, phenylalanine using aptamer, and sodium using ion-selective membrane was achieved. In addition, using a dual coil system, each coil designed to resonate at a distinct frequency, multiplexed sensing was demonstrated in complex phantom environments, mimicking potential clinical applications.

TI Research Progress in Precision Fabrication of Optoelectronic Integrated Neural Microprobes (Invited)
SO LASER & OPTOELECTRONICS PROGRESS
LA Chinese
DT Article
DE optoelectronic integration; neural microprobe; micro-nano fabrication; soft material
ID silicon probes; brain; systems
AB Significance Optoelectronic-integrated neural microprobes are revolutionizing both fundamental and translational neuroscience by enabling precise, bidirectional access to neural circuits with cellular-to meso-scale resolution. Integrating high-density electrodes, cellular-level optical actuation/readout, and local chemical input/output empowers these systems to establish causality in neural circuitry and to advance clinically relevant closed-loop interventions. Significant challenges persist, however, in achieving reliable long-term interfaces within the soft, vascularized, and dynamically moving brain tissue. Key hurdles include mechanical and hydration mismatches, shear stress from micromotion, immune encapsulation, and the difficulty of miniaturizing and co-packaging multiple functional modalities without compromising device footprint or thermal load. This review synthesizes recent progress in precision fabrication that addresses these constraints across three complementary device families: rigid silicon-based shanks, soft thin-film probes, and ultrafine fiber-like probes. It further highlights how convergent innovations in materials science and micro-nano manufacturing are steering the field toward minimally invasive, multimodal, and clinically translatable brain-machine interfaces. Progress Rigid silicon-based probes leverage mature MEMS foundries to achieve micrometer-scale patterning, wafer-level yields, and tight optoelectronic integration. While early microwires provided initial unit access, they suffered from placement variability and poor impedance scaling. Subsequent planar silicon shanks enabled scalable, multiplexed recording (Fig. 2(a), (b)). Recent advances incorporate reconfigurable silicon nitride (SiN) photonic switches directly alongside high-density electrode arrays. This design delivers cell-specific optical stimulation while recording from proximal units, thereby confining the activation volume and enabling on-chip beam steering without external fibers (Fig. 2(c)). Parallel efforts embed microfluidic conduits to couple pharmacology with electrophysiology and optogenetics, creating genuinely trimodal shanks for investigating long-range circuits via multi-shank assemblies (Fig. 2(d)). Persistent limitations include the stark mismatch in elastic modulus and surface chemistry with brain parenchyma, insertion-induced vascular injury, and chronic gliosis, which degrades the signal-to-noise ratio and causes unit yield to drift over time. Flexible thin-film probes address these biocompatibility issues by matching tissue-level mechanics while retaining microfabrication precision. Architectured designs, such as mesh and injectable "tassel" electrodes, utilize capillarity-guided self-assembly for minimally invasive insertion, distributing recording sites across local microdomains upon deployment. High-precision 3D printing of conductive polymer inks has been used to fabricate neural electrodes, which are subsequently implanted with temporary shuttles for stable chronic recording (Fig. 3(a)). Additive techniques like direct ink writing (DIW) now pattern conductive hydrogels and elastomers into porous, stretchable interfaces. Their bending stiffness approaches that of neural tissue, reducing shear from micromotion and permitting bidirectional molecular transport across the device-tissue boundary (Fig. 3(b)). Catheter-deliverable micro-endovascular (MEV) ribbons can navigate sub-100-mu m vessels and record single-unit activity through the vessel wall, enabling deep-brain access without a craniotomy while preserving the blood-brain barrier (Fig. 3(c)). The rolling of soft electronics (ROSE) technique deterministically transforms 2D multilayer architectures into continuous 3D shanks with programmable, Archimedean spirals, integrating hundreds of low-impedance PEDOT:PSS sites and demonstrating a significantly reduced microglial response upon primate implantation (Fig. 4). Complementary material advances include reduced-graphene-oxide (rGO) interfaces formed as 3D porous scaffolds or ultra-thin 2D films, drastically lowering impedance (similar to 0.3-1 k Omega at 1 kHz) while boosting charge storage capacity and enabling stable chronic recordings via intimate neuron-electrode interpenetration (Fig. 5). The primary trade-off for thin-film designs remains the insertion challenge posed by extreme flexibility, often necessitating bioresorbable shuttles. Furthermore, the seamless integration of bright micro-emitters, low-loss waveguides, and robust fluidics without volumetric penalty is an ongoing challenge. Fiber-like probes utilize thermal drawing, non-planar microfabrication, and materials co-processing to integrate optical waveguides, microelectrodes, and microfluidic channels within polymer fibers with diameters tapering below 200 mu m. These multifunctional fibers, fabricated in a single draw, enable opsin delivery, optical stimulation, drug injection, and electrical recording in freely behaving animals (Fig. 6(a), (b)). Incorporating hydrogel domains enhances hydration and biochemical affinity, improving chronic biocompatibility (Fig. 6(c)). Recent polymer stack designs consolidate six functions-electrophysiology, optical stimulation/photometry, voltammetric neurochemical sensing, and fluidic delivery-within a single, MRI-compatible fiber, allowing concurrent fMRI and deep-brain electro-opto-chemical interrogation with minimal susceptibility artifacts (Fig. 6(d)). Moving beyond static implants, an earthworm-inspired, magnetically steerable micro-fiber (NeuroWorm) integrates dozens of longitudinal electrodes and strain sensors within an ultracompliant SEBS-metal composite, enabling dynamic adaptation to evolving circuit dynamics (Fig. 6(e)). To mitigate photoelectric artifacts in ultracompact volumes, tapered "fibertrodes" conformally pattern platinum (Pt) microelectrodes on curved fiber surfaces using focused-ion-beam (FIB) and two-photon polymerization (2PP). This creates angled side-emitting optical windows that illuminate away from the recording rings, suppressing artifacts below the noise floor during millisecond pulses (Fig. 7(a), (b)). In general, fiber platforms offer advantages in footprint, conformability to curved surfaces, and MRI compatibility. However, thermal-drawing constraints limit the use of thermally sensitive bio-materials, while enhancing sidewall site density and long-term mechanical anchoring remain active research areas. Conclusions and Prospects Precision fabrication is rapidly advancing neural microprobes toward devices that are soft, miniature, and intelligent, capable of chronic, multimodal, and artifact-free operation in complex, dynamic tissue. Immediate research priorities encompass: 1) novel materials that couple high conductivity with brain-like mechanics and controlled biodegradability to mitigate long-term foreign-body response; 2) hybrid manufacturing that merges high-fidelity lithography with 3D printing, non-planar patterning, and self-assembly to achieve dense 3D site distributions; 3) deeper multimodal integration spanning optics, electrophysiology, electrochemistry, and microfluidics within thermally and volumetrically efficient architectures; 4) embedded closed-loop intelligence, where on-probe sensing, low-noise amplification, and control algorithms drive adaptive stimulation. Successful translation will also depend on standardized packaging and connectorization, robust wireless power and data links, and rigorous safety/regulatory pathways, including long-term biostability data. Ultimately, as optoelectronic integration matures and soft intelligent materials converge with scalable micro-nano manufacturing, neural microprobes are poised to transition from powerful research tools to clinically viable neurotherapeutic platforms. This evolution promises targeted, multimodal interventions for a range of neurological and psychiatric disorders, including movement disorders, epilepsy, and cognitive deficits. The field is entering an era where precision fabrication is not merely an enabler but the central driver of scientific discovery and therapeutic innovation.

TI Natural cellulose-based flexible recyclable multifunctional sensor
SO NANO ENERGY
LA English
DT Article
DE MEMS; Flexible electronics; Multifunctional sensing; Natural cellulose; Recycling sensor
AB With the rapid development of Internet of Things (IoT) technology and the improvement of personal health awareness, flexible sensors play an irreplaceable role in heart rate detection, respiration monitoring, and many other healthcare applications. To meet the requirement of the high utilization rate of spaces and low cost at the same time, increasing attention has been focused on the multifunctional flexible sensors. However, certain issues of the traditional flexible sensors based on artificial polymers such as complex manufacturing processes, use of toxic reagents and inability to recycle limit their popularity for daily consumption scenarios. Herein, we proposed an easily recyclable multifunctional sensor fabricated from the natural cellulose-based flexible piezoelectric (NCFP) composite film. This NCFP film combines the moisture sensitivity of natural cellulose, piezoelectric effect and variable permittivity of barium titanate (BTO) nanoparticles, exhibiting advantageous performance in terms of pressure, temperature and humidity sensing response. Meanwhile, after five recycling processes, NCFP film remains at a high level of consistency. Furthermore, the multifunctional sensor based on NCFP film was successfully applied for finger pressure detection, finger temperature detection and respiration monitoring. Consequently, the findings are promising for flexible, low-cost and sustainable multifunctional wearable devices.

TI Generalized Rodrigues parameters-based fast in-flight alignment approaches with GNSS assistance for SUAV
SO AEROSPACE SCIENCE AND TECHNOLOGY
LA English
DT Article
DE In-flight alignment; Generalized Rodrigues parameters; SINS error model; SUAV; Kalman filter
ID rapid transfer alignment; initial alignment; gps; navigation; quaternion; attitude; systems; model; sins
AB With the increasing diversification of small unmanned aerial vehicle (SUAV) launch methods, airdrops have emerged as a critical approach for large-scale deployment. In-flight alignment (IFA) is essential for enhancing airdrop efficiency and flexibility without relying on additional launch platform information. This paper proposes a novel strapdown inertial navigation system (SINS) error model based on generalized Rodrigues parameters (GRPs) to improve IFA performance with global navigation satellite system (GNSS) assistance. A uniform GRPsbased nonlinear error model for attitude and velocity is developed, which allows the filter to propagate GRPs directly without quaternion transformations, enhancing robustness and stability. An unscented Kalman filter (UKF)-based GNSS-aided IFA approach is constructed using indirect filtering. To reduce computational complexity, a GRPs-based linear error model is derived, leveraging micro-electro-mechanical systems (MEMS) inertial measurement unit (IMU) characteristics and indirect filtering principles, and a Kalman filter (KF)-based GNSS-aided IFA approach is established. Observability analysis is conducted under six flight maneuver modes, followed by hardware-in-the-loop (HIL) simulations and field flight tests. Results show that the proposed models outperform existing linear psi-angle-based, nonlinear Euler angle-based, and nonlinear quaternion-based models, achieving alignment errors below 1.5 degrees within 6 seconds under high maneuverability, with alignment accuracy under 0.7 degrees. Field tests further validate the superiority of the approaches. The GRPs-based linear model reduces computational complexity by 60 % while maintaining comparable alignment performance, making it suitable for resource-constrained SUAV onboard processors.

TI Fully integrated AI-enhanced flexible wearable sensor for real-time movement evaluation and table tennis training
SO BIOSENSORS & BIOELECTRONICS
LA English
DT Article
DE Flexible wearable sensor; Wearable electronics; Movement evaluation; Spatiotemporal analysis; Human motion monitoring
ID strain sensors; platform; recognition; electronics; device
AB Wearable electronic devices are crucial for continuous health monitoring and human-computer interaction, yet achieving full integration, flexibility, and high-fidelity accuracy for biomechanical assessment remains challenging. Here, we report a thin, skin-conformal flexible inertial sensing platform for accurate motion capture and spatiotemporal movement assessment. High-precision micro-electromechanical systems (MEMS) inertial sensors are integrated onto a customized flexible printed circuit and encapsulated within a medical-grade adhesive patch for direct epidermal mounting. The standalone device wirelessly transmits acceleration, angular velocity, and orientation data with low power consumption, achieving static angular errors below 0.14 degrees and dynamic drift of 6.21 degrees during continuous motion. We implemented a spatiotemporal assessment framework that decouples spatial posture accuracy from temporal execution order using limited dynamic time warping (LDTW) and multiple sequence alignment (MAFFT). Using 60 expert demonstrations of table tennis forehand attacks, evaluation models were constructed with maximum spatial tolerance of 7.39 degrees and 14 consensus temporal landmarks. A sensor network enabled motion capture at key anatomical locations, achieving overall RMSE of 11.54 +/- 8.46 degrees against optical motion capture. In a training validation study (n = 20), novices using automated sensor-based guidance achieved 41.67% improvement in movement quality, comparable to expert coaching (55.75%, p = 0.175). The platform demonstrated high-accuracy action recognition (99.86%), trajectory estimation, and real-time movement assessment, supporting its potential for motion analysis in sports training, rehabilitation, and personalized healthcare.

TI Casimir arc-plate geometry: computational analysis of thickness constraints for gold and silver nanomembranes in MEMS applications
SO JOURNAL OF MICROMECHANICS AND MICROENGINEERING
LA English
DT Article
DE Casimir force; MEMS; proximity force approximation; nanomembrane mechanics; geometric effects; material dependence.
ID force
AB A theoretical analysis of the Casimir interaction between an arc and plate is conducted, which remains unexplored despite its relevance to micro-electro-mechanical systems (MEMS) fabrication. The configuration consists of a rigid finite plate and a flexible curved nanomembrane, with radius 100 mu m, initially concave toward the rigid plate. The maximum thickness is evaluated for which the nanomembrane undergoes a change in curvature: from concave to convex with respect to the plate, due to the Casimir interaction. The Casimir energy for a curved surface is derived using the proximity force approximation (PFA) with next-to-leading-order (NTLO) corrections. Kirchhoff-Love theory for a thin isotropic plate of constant thickness is used to estimate the bending energy. Material-dependent effects on the Casimir interaction are evaluated by comparing Au and Ag plates. The maximum thickness is derived where U-Casimir > U-bending for distances in the range of 0.1-1 mu m. Results show curvature reversal occurs for nanomembranes with nanoscale thicknesses at the studied distances. Silver nanomembranes tolerate greater thickness than gold nanomembranes due to material-dependent properties. Comparison between NTLO-corrected PFA and perturbative PFA confirms the accuracy of the NTLO approach. The Casimir arc-to-plate geometry in MEMS enables Casimir-based actuation, enhances devices reliability, and prevents stiction. These findings provide thickness constraints for MEMS design and performance, accounting for the Casimir force.

TI Stretchable MEMS neural electrodes with a honeycomb topology for impact-resilient and stable chronic recording
SO SENSORS AND ACTUATORS A-PHYSICAL
LA English
DT Article
DE Flexible neural electrode; Deep brain recording; Micromotion mitigation; Honeycomb mesh topology; Iridium oxide modification; Chronic stability; Negative Poisson's ratio structure
ID probes
AB Mechanical mismatch and micromotion-induced drift limit the long-term stability of flexible neural electrodes for deep-brain recording. We present a probe that integrates a negative-Poisson-ratio structure in a honeycomb mesh topology with iridium oxide (IrOx) modification of recording sites to improve mechanical anchoring and electrochemical performance. The negative Poisson's ratio mesh electrode (NPME) was fabricated via wafer-scale polyimide-Au-polyimide microfabrication with IrOx-modified. Mechanical behaviours were assessed through uniaxial tensile tests in 0.8% agarose and finite-element modelling to map strain shielding, lateral-expansion-induced contact pressure, and equivalent bending stiffness. Stereotaxic implantation in rats enabled chronic local field potential and single-unit recordings with spike clustering and signal-to-noise ratio quantification. Magnetic resonance imaging was used to track the in vivo probe position. The NPME achieved > 70% stretchability and an equivalent bending stiffness more than an order of magnitude lower than conventional solid electrodes, enabling superior conformability and reduced insertion trauma. Simulations showed that tensile loading induced lateral expansion, enhancing tissue contact and anchorage. IrOx modification increased surface roughness and reduced 1 kHz impedance by over an order of magnitude relative to bare Au, remaining stable over six weeks in vivo. In vivo rat experiments demonstrated chronic stability for > 44 days with stable single-unit isolation and high SNR; MRI shows no observable significant displacement and immunofluorescence indicated a small immune response. This work demonstrates that combining the structural mechanics with IrOx-modified interfaces yields a chronically stable, mechanically adaptive, and electrically efficient deep-brain interface, advancing next-generation flexible neural electrodes for micromotion mitigation and deep-brain recording.

TI Soft Magnetite-PEDOT:PSS Composite Microactuator Fabrication via Meniscus-Assisted 3D Printing
SO ADVANCED MATERIALS TECHNOLOGIES
LA English
DT Article
DE magnetite; meniscus-evaporation fabrication; microactuator; PEDOT:PSS; soft robotic finger
ID microrobot; design
AB Soft microrobots are gaining interest in various fields including microelectromechanical systems (MEMS), bioelectronics, and soft robotics due to their adaptable and compliant motion at the microscale. Magnetic soft microactuators often require a balance between mechanical robustness and magnetic responsiveness, which can be challenging to achieve simultaneously. Here, a magnetically controlled soft micro actuator based on a composite of magnetite and PEDOT:PSS (poly(3,4-ethylenedioxythiophene)) is presented, fabricated using a meniscus-assisted microfabrication technique. In this design, the PEDOT:PSS layer provides structural flexibility while the magnetite layer enables precise magnetic actuation. The fabrication method ensures high spatial precision and surface compatibility, allowing the actuator to be integrated onto various substrates. Experimental results confirm stable, reversible, and tunable motion under an external magnetic field, with a linear displacement response of 0.20 degrees/V. The actuator's flexibility and reliability make it promising for soft robotic manipulation, bioelectronics, and micromanipulation applications. This study paves the way for advanced, magnetically responsive microactuators in next-generation MEMS and biomedical systems.

TI Magnetic Properties of Nd-Fe-B Single Dots Fabricated Using the PLD-LIFT Method
SO IEEE MAGNETICS LETTERS
LA English
DT Article
DE Magnetic hysteresis; Substrates; Microelectromechanical systems; Coercive force; Perpendicular magnetic anisotropy; Magnetic resonance imaging; Magnetic properties; Soft magnetic materials; Saturation magnetization; Receivers; Neodymium compounds; Hard magnetic materials; Nd-Fe-B dot; laser-induced forward transfer; low-temperature process; microelectromechanical systems
AB Single-dot Nd-Fe-B micromagnets were fabricated using the pulsed laser deposition-laser-induced forward transfer (PLD-LIFT) technique, and their magnetic properties were systematically examined. Hysteresis measurements with a vibrating sample magneto meter revealed that the coercivity (H-c) was nearly independent of laser power, whereas an increased defocus rate (DF rate) enhanced H-c up to 340 kA/m. Scanning electron microscopy and cross-sectional scanning transmission electron microscopy analyses revealed that each dot comprises grains ranging from submicrometer to micrometer scale. Within these grains, an Nd2Fe14B core is encapsulated by an Fe-rich matrix containing dispersed Nd oxides. The thickness of this Fe-rich outer shell modifies the exchange pathway at the Nd2Fe14B/Fe interface, giving rise to the characteristic two-step demagnetization. Guided by these observations, a simplified Nd2Fe14B/alpha-Fe core-shell model was developed and evaluated through micromagnetic simulations, which successfully reproduced the stepwise reversal and clarified DF's role in suppressing soft-phase connectivity and improving loop squareness. Collectively, these findings identify DF rate as the dominant processing parameter and provide practical guidelines for tailoring PLD-LIFT Nd-Fe-B micromagnets toward microelectromechanical systems applications.

TI Identification of Flexible Joint Multibody Dynamic Models for Machine Tool Feed Drive Assemblies Via Inertial Measurement Unit and Computer Numerical Control Data
SO JOURNAL OF MANUFACTURING SCIENCE AND ENGINEERING-TRANSACTIONS OF THE ASME
LA English
DT Article
DE control and automation; machine tool dynamics; modeling and simulation; system identification; inertial measurement unit (IMU)
ID performance
AB High-accuracy modeling of machine tool dynamics is essential for advanced process planning and monitoring. However, modeling high-speed multi-axis machines is challenging due to the inherent coupled and nonlinear multibody dynamics and structural flexibility. This complex modeling task is addressed by a new approach in which the control dynamics and the open-loop plant dynamics are characterized by a multiple-input and multiple-output (MIMO) linear time-invariant (LTI) system coupled with a generalized disturbance, which is able to capture the open-loop coupled nonlinear dynamics. As a case study, different machine tool topologies of a flexible linear drive coupled with a rotary drive are systematically analyzed using the proposed modeling approach. The identification procedure for the proposed method requires capturing the internal structural vibration between the drives. This article also presents a method to reconstruct the internal structural vibration using data from the embedded encoders as well as a low-cost microelectromechanical systems (MEMS) inertial measurement unit (IMU) mounted on the machine table. This modeling-building approach is nonintrusive and practical for industrial implementation. The experimental validation shows a 2-6% error in predicting the tracking error and motor force/torque. Especially, the vibratory inter-axis coupling effect and posture-dependency are accurately predicted.

TI Reducing debris in laser transfer printing utilizing a TiOx nanofilm for high-quality additive manufacturing of microscale copper structures
SO JOURNAL OF MATERIALS PROCESSING TECHNOLOGY
LA English
DT Article
DE Laser printing; Microscale additive manufacturing; Laser-induced forward transfer; Copper structures; Nanofilms
ID metals; viscosity; oxidation; dynamics; oxides
AB Laser-induced forward transfer (LIFT) with solid metal film donors is a contactless, nozzle-free, additive manufacturing technique for microscale metallic structures. However, excessive debris and satellite droplets generated during transfer can form porous and less-accurate structures. In this study, we introduce a TiOx nanofilm as an adhesion and buffer layer to address these issues. Experimental results show that a TiOx layer of optimized thickness can enhance the transfer stability, increase particle size, and reduce debris and satellite droplets. These improvements facilitate the formation of low-porosity, smooth-surface, and high-aspect-ratio pillar structures, whose yield strength and elastic modulus reach 258.3 MPa and 24.7 GPa, respectively. The underlying mechanisms are investigated by measuring the incubation time and droplet velocity in LIFT, by recording a transient electrical signal generated when a droplet lands on the receiver. The results indicate that the addition of a TiOx layer delays droplet departure and reduces droplet velocity. These effects are attributed to the higher stability and enhanced adhesion of the TiOx-Cu interface, compared to that of the glass-Cu interface, which reduces the propulsive pressure generated in LIFT. Using the optimized donor films, various sophisticated pillar arrays are fabricated, including a copper pillar of diameter 7 & micro;m and aspect ratio of 571:1, and a "pillar skyscraper" composed of 220 micropillars. This work highlights the crucial role of the donor interface in LIFT and provides a new strategy for debris reduction, benefiting the widespread applications of LIFT in microelectromechanical systems, microfluidic chips, and flexible electronic devices.

TI Simultaneous Enhancement of Remanence and Coercivity in Anisotropic Nd-Fe-B Thick Films via Fe Composite and Tb Diffusion Layers
SO ACS APPLIED MATERIALS & INTERFACES
LA English
DT Article
DE Nd-Fe-B thick film; magneticproperties; long-range coupling; Tb diffusion; coercivitymechanism
ID grain-boundary diffusion; magnetic-property; reversal; 1st-order
AB Modulations on the microstructure and magnetic properties of Nd-Fe-B thick films are essential for applications in microelectromechanical systems. In Nd-Fe-B/Fe multilayers, strong long-range dipolar interactions between hard and soft magnetic phases lead to the enhancement of remanence but result in lower coercivity. In this work, Tb diffusion layers are integrated into Nd-Fe-B/Ta/Fe multilayer thick films to systematically investigate the magnetic properties and coercivity mechanism. The results show that the introduction of Tb diffusion layers in Nd-Fe-B/Ta/Fe multilayers significantly enhances both remanence and coercivity, increasing the maximum energy product from 25.2 to 36.2 MGOe. Microstructural characterization and magnetization reversal analysis reveal that the multilayer structure improves crystallinity and promotes the c-axis texture of the main magnetic phase, while the long-range dipolar interaction between Fe and Nd-Fe-B layers effectively enhances the overall magnetization. Meanwhile, Tb diffusion strengthens domain wall pinning in the main magnetic phase, suppressing the inhomogeneous magnetization reversal induced by the Fe composite layers. Consequently, a simultaneous enhancement of the remanence and coercivity is achieved. This study provides insights into preparation strategies for high-performance Nd-Fe-B thick films.

TI Dual-diaphragm structural design and validation for overload protection in MEMS piezoresistive absolute pressure sensors
SO JOURNAL OF MICROMECHANICS AND MICROENGINEERING
LA English
DT Article
DE MEMS piezoresistive absolute pressure sensor; dual-diaphragm overload-protection structure; deep-sea pressure measurement; finite element analysis; performance testing
ID stability
AB This study presents a package-level dual-diaphragm metal housing that improves overload resistance and operational stability of micro-electro-mechanical system piezoresistive absolute pressure sensors for deep-sea environments, targeting hydrostatic pressures up to 70 MPa (similar to 7000 m water depth). To overcome limitations of conventional single-diaphragm designs, we propose a dual-diaphragm overload protection structure integrating a cooperative 'central diaphragm-limiting contour body' mechanism for self-limiting deformation under overload, without modifying the sensing chip or its microassembly. Theoretical analysis and COMSOL finite-element simulations compared 316 l stainless steel and TC4 titanium alloy diaphragms under identical geometry and boundary conditions, using elastic properties without parameter normalization. TC4 exhibits greater elastic deflection and lower stress, improving pressure sensitivity while maintaining a sufficient safety margin. Parametric optimization yielded an 18.4 mm-diameter, 50 mu m-thick diaphragm geometry that balances flexibility and strength. Simulations confirm that at 70 MPa the maximum diaphragm stress remains below yield, and at 25 MPa the central-diaphragm displacement matches the designed 36 mu m clearance, validating the cooperative limiting mechanism. A prototype sensor employing a 0-3 MPa piezoresistive absolute pressure chip was fabricated to verify the proposed structure. At 25 degrees C, static calibration within 0-3 MPa yields a full-scale output of 16.21 mV and a normalized sensitivity of 0.00 108 (mV/V)/kPa, with nonlinearity, hysteresis and repeatability errors of 0.028%FS, 0.049%FS and 0.126%FS, corresponding to an overall static accuracy of 0.136%FS (<= 0.3%FS). Dynamic monitoring under external pressure of 3-22 MPa gives a comprehensive error of 0.348%FS, and repeated overload tests up to 70 MPa lead to only a slight increase in hysteresis while preserving an overall accuracy of 0.215%FS, with no oil leakage or structural damage. All characterizations reported here were conducted at 25 degrees C; systematic temperature-dependent and extended-duration drift/stability tests are identified as future work. These results demonstrate robust overload protection for deep-sea pressure sensors.

TI Advanced modeling of magneto-thermoelastic interactions in rotating viscoelastic rods using Caputo-Fabrizio fractional derivatives and nonlocal heat conduction with a correlating length
SO INTERNATIONAL JOURNAL OF MECHANICS AND MATERIALS IN DESIGN
LA English
DT Article
DE Caputo-Fabrizio derivative; Nonlocal heat conduction; Size-dependent effects; Hereditary characteristics; Rotational dynamics; Kelvin-Voigt model
ID thermoelasticity; behavior; lequation
AB Conventional thermoelastic models fail to capture the coupled size-dependent, memory-dependent, and high-frequency dynamics inherent in rotating viscoelastic nanostructures under multi-physics loads. To bridge this gap, this study introduces the first unified theoretical model that simultaneously incorporates Eringen's nonlocal elasticity, Guyer-Krumhansl nonlocal heat conduction with a thermal length-scale parameter, the non-singular Caputo-Fabrizio fractional derivative, and a fractional Lord-Shulman/Moore-Gibson-Thompson thermoelastic framework. Applied to a finite rotating viscoelastic Kelvin-Voigt rod under a transverse magnetic field and a moving heat source, the unified formulation captures size-dependent mechanical behavior, nonlocal thermal diffusion, hereditary memory effects, finite thermal wave speed, Lorentz forces, and rotational dynamics in a single consistent framework. Results show that mechanical and thermal nonlocality dramatically smooth temperature gradients, lower peak temperatures, reduce displacement amplitudes, and virtually eliminate stress concentrations compared to classical local theories. The Caputo-Fabrizio derivative delivers smoother, more physically realistic memory-dependent responses than both the integer-order case and the traditional singular-kernel Caputo derivative. Rotation speed, magnetic field intensity, heat-source velocity, and fractional order strongly influence wave propagation and field distributions. The model provides an accurate predictive tool for rotating micro/nano rods in MEMS/NEMS, high-speed laser processing of viscoelastic nanostructures, magnetically controlled smart composites, aerospace components under combined thermal-rotational-electromagnetic loading, and advanced flexible electronics where precise management of size-dependent thermal stresses is critical. It establishes a robust, physically consistent foundation for designing and optimizing modern viscoelastic nanostructures subjected to extreme multi-physics environments.

TI Design of a Distributed Passive Micro-Magnetic Stimulator for Targeted Multi-Brain Region Neuromodulation
SO IEEE TRANSACTIONS ON BIOMEDICAL ENGINEERING
LA English
DT Article
DE Coils; Implants; Wireless communication; Collaboration; Biomedical engineering; Synchronization; Regulation; Frequency division multiaccess; Downlink; Uplink; Passive micro-magnetic stimulation; MEMS micro-coil; distributed structure; collaborative neuro-regulation; neural circuit
ID wireless power transmission; spinal-cord; potentiation; rectifier
AB The multi-channel synchronous stimulator, aimed at achieving efficient and precise neural regulation, typically utilizes a monolithic microelectrode array structure. However, this structure limits the flexibility of electrode placement and the expansion to a large number of nodes, particularly in discontinuous locations. To address this, this paper designs a distributed passive micro-magnetic stimulation (DP-mu MS) neuro-regulation device with multi-brain region collaborative stimulation functionality. It consists of an external Emission coil, Recognition coil, and multiple MEMS micro-coil (a collection unit of Stimulation coils and Addressing coils) distributed at different positions within the body. The DP-mu MS utilizes magnetic coupling technology to achieve collaborative operation among the coils. In the downlink, the Stimulation coils couple with the energy transmitted by the Emission coil to achieve magnetic stimulation, and the rectifier circuit collects energy to power the Addressing coils. In the uplink, each Addressing coil returns a unique frequency signal using Frequency Division Multiple Access (FDMA), and the Recognition coil demodulates the frequency to identify the ID of the micro-coil, enabling the monitoring of the collaborative working status of each micro-coil. Based on this, biological experiments were conducted to verify the functionality of DP-mu MS on the neural circuits in the hippocampal region. The results indicated that multi-point collaborative magnetic stimulation enhanced long-term potentiation (LTP) of the Schaffer-CA1 synapse more effectively than single-point stimulation. This study provides a solution for multi-brain region targeted therapy for neurological diseases.

TI A Novel Cost-Effective MIMO Architecture With Ray Antenna Array for Enhanced Wireless Communication Performance
SO IEEE TRANSACTIONS ON WIRELESS COMMUNICATIONS
LA English
DT Article
DE Ray antenna array (RAA); high-frequency systems; cost-effective antenna architecture; flexible beamforming; Ray antenna array (RAA); high-frequency systems; cost-effective antenna architecture; flexible beamforming
ID rf-mems switches
AB This paper proposes a novel multi-antenna architecture, termed ray antenna array (RAA), which practically enables flexible beamforming and also enhances wireless communication performance for high-frequency systems in a cost-effective manner. RAA consists of a large number of inexpensive antenna elements and a few radio frequency (RF) chains. These antenna elements are arranged in a novel ray-like structure, where each ray corresponds to one simple uniform linear array (sULA) with a carefully designed orientation. The antenna elements within each sULA are directly connected, so that each sULA is able to form a beam towards a direction matching the ray orientation without relying on any analog or digital beamforming. By further designing a ray selection network (RSN), appropriate sULAs are selected and connected to the RF chains. Compared to conventional multi-antenna architectures such as the uniform linear array (ULA) with hybrid analog/digital beamforming (HBF), the proposed RAA enjoys four appealing advantages: (i) finer and uniform angular resolution for all signal directions; (ii) enhanced beamforming gain by using antenna elements with higher directivity, as each sULA is only responsible for a small portion of the total angle coverage range; (iii) dramatically reduced hardware cost since no phase shifters are required, which are expensive and difficult to design in high-frequency systems such as millimeter wave (mmWave) and Terahertz (THz) systems; (iv) beam squint free, enabling stable beamforming performance in high-frequency wideband communications. To validate such advantages, we first present the input-output mathematical model for RAA-based wireless communications. Efficient algorithms for joint RAA beamforming and ray selection are then proposed for single-user and multi-user RAA-based wireless communications. Simulation results demonstrate that RAA achieves superior performance compared to the conventional ULA with HBF, while significantly reducing hardware cost.

TI Higher Order Dynamic Modeling and Robust Optimization of Fully Decoupled MEMS Gyroscope With Fabrication Errors
SO IEEE SENSORS JOURNAL
LA English
DT Article
DE Gyroscopes; Microelectromechanical systems; Springs; Couplings; Sensitivity; Microstructure; Force; Optimization; Electrostatics; Intelligent sensors; Dynamic modeling; micro-electromechanical systems (MEMS) fabrication error; MEMS gyroscope; quadrature output; robust optimization
ID vibratory gyroscope; design
AB The fully decoupled configurations integrating high-aspect-ratio flexible beams and proof masses are a widely adopted design in micro-electromechanical systems (MEMS) vibratory gyroscopes. However, such structures are extremely sensitive to microfabrication errors, particularly beamwidth deviations. To address this issue, this article proposes a high-order nonlinear dynamic modeling method and a robust optimization strategy for fully decoupled MEMS gyroscopes, explicitly accounting for microfabrication errors. Nonlinear stiffness expressions are derived for double-folded beams, V-shaped coupling beams, and composite beams, and a high-order dynamic model is established incorporating both geometric and electrostatic nonlinearities. Theoretical natural frequencies agree well with finite element simulations, with deviations below 3.3%. Assuming that all beamwidth deviations are normally distributed, the direct probability integration method (DPIM) is used to quantify gyroscope performance uncertainties in mechanical sensitivity and quadrature output. Robust optimization is conducted using beam structural parameters and drive voltage as design variables, aiming to improve sensitivity while minimizing deviations in sensitivity and quadrature output. The optimized gyroscope structure demonstrates strong robustness against beamwidth deviations, achieving a 3.75 times improvement in sensitivity and a 31% reduction in quadrature output. The 1 sigma standard deviation of sensitivity and quadrature output is significantly reduced by 78.1% and 40.2%, respectively, effectively suppressing the impact of fabrication errors on device performance.
